### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The velocity at which $6$ kg mass (shown in figure) strikes the ground when it is released from a height of $6$ m above the ground is __________ m/s. Assume pulley is massless and string is light and inextensible. (Take g $= 10$ m/s$^2$)
<img style="width: 200px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/06APRS2__q44_d1.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** $7.74$
- **(B)** $7.20$
- **(C)** $6.55$
- **(D)** $4.50$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $7.74$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the acceleration of the system be $a$ and the tension in the string be $T$.

The equations of motion for the two masses are:
$6g - T = 6a$
$T - 2g = 2a$

Adding the two equations, we get:
$4g = 8a$
$\Rightarrow a = \dfrac{4g}{8} = \dfrac{g}{2}$

Substituting $g = 10 \text{ m/s}^2$, the acceleration is:
$a = \dfrac{10}{2} = 5 \text{ m/s}^2$

The $6 \text{ kg}$ mass is released from rest ($u = 0$) and travels a downward distance $s = 6 \text{ m}$.

Using the kinematic equation $v^2 = u^2 + 2as$:
$v^2 = 0 + 2 \times 5 \times 6$
$v^2 = 60$
$v = \sqrt{60} \approx 7.74 \text{ m/s}$

Answer: $7.74$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A body of mass $1$ kg moves along a straight line with a velocity $v = 2x^2$. The work done by the body during displacement from $x = 0$ to $5$ m is __________ J.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0$
- **(B)** $250$
- **(C)** $1250$
- **(D)** $1000$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1250$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given mass $m = 1$ kg and velocity $v = 2x^2$.

According to the work-energy theorem, the work done by the net force is equal to the change in kinetic energy of the body.

$W = \Delta K = K_f - K_i$

At $x = 0$ m, initial velocity $v_i = 2(0)^2 = 0$ m/s.

At $x = 5$ m, final velocity $v_f = 2(5)^2 = 50$ m/s.

Initial kinetic energy $K_i = \dfrac{1}{2} m v_i^2 = 0$ J.

Final kinetic energy $K_f = \dfrac{1}{2} m v_f^2 = \dfrac{1}{2} \times 1 \times (50)^2 = 1250$ J.

Work done $W = 1250 - 0 = 1250$ J.

Answer: $1250$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A smooth inclined plane ends in a vertical circular loop, as shown in the figure. A small body is released from height $h$ as shown. If the body exerts a force of three times its weight on the plane at the highest point of circle then the height $h = \alpha R$. The value of $\alpha$ is _______.
<img style="width: 250px" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_april_watermarked/06APRS1__q32_d1.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $3$
- **(D)** $6$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $v$ be the velocity of the body at the highest point of the circular loop.

At the highest point, the forces acting on the body are its weight $mg$ (downwards) and the normal reaction $N$ (downwards). The net downward force provides the necessary centripetal acceleration:

$N + mg = \dfrac{mv^2}{R}$

Given that the body exerts a force equal to three times its weight on the track at the highest point, we have $N = 3mg$. Substituting this into the equation:

$3mg + mg = \dfrac{mv^2}{R}$

$4mg = \dfrac{mv^2}{R} \Rightarrow v^2 = 4gR$

Applying the principle of conservation of mechanical energy between the point of release and the highest point of the loop (which is at a height of $2R$ from the bottom):

$mgh = mg(2R) + \dfrac{1}{2}mv^2$

Substituting $v^2 = 4gR$ into the energy equation:

$mgh = 2mgR + \dfrac{1}{2}m(4gR)$

$mgh = 2mgR + 2mgR = 4mgR$

Therefore, $h = 4R$.

Comparing this with the given relation $h = \alpha R$, we get $\alpha = 4$.

Answer: $4$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A spherical ball of mass $2$ kg falls from a height of $10$ m and is brought to rest after penetrating $10$ cm into sand. The average force exerted by sand on the ball is _______ N. (Take $g = 10$ m/s$^2$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1980$
- **(B)** $2020$
- **(C)** $2000$
- **(D)** $1000$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2020$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using the work-energy theorem, the total work done on the ball is equal to its change in kinetic energy.

Since the ball starts from rest and finally comes to rest, the change in kinetic energy is zero.

$W_{\text{gravity}} + W_{\text{sand}} = \Delta K = 0$

The work done by gravity is over the total distance $h + d$, and the work done by the resistive force of the sand $F$ is over the distance $d$.

$mg(h + d) - Fd = 0$

$F = \dfrac{mg(h + d)}{d}$

Given $m = 2$ kg, $g = 10$ m/s$^2$, $h = 10$ m, and $d = 10$ cm $= 0.1$ m:

$F = \dfrac{2 \times 10 \times (10 + 0.1)}{0.1}$

$F = \dfrac{20 \times 10.1}{0.1}$

$F = 20 \times 101 = 2020$ N

Answer: $2020$

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A bead $P$ sliding on a frictionless semi-circular string $(A C B)$ and it is at point $S$ at $t =0$ and at this instant the horizontal component of its velocity is $v$. Another bead $Q$ of the same mass as $P$ is ejected from point $A$ at $t=0$ along the horizontal string $A B$, with the speed $v$, friction between the beads and the respective strings may be neglected in both cases. Let $t_{P}$ and $t_{Q}$ be the respective times taken by beads $P$ and $Q$ to reach the point $B$, then the relation between $t_{P}$ and $t_{Q}$ is 
 <img style="width:200px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/23S2_q_29_1.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** ${ }^{t_{P} < t_{Q}}$
- **(B)** ${ }^{t_{P}>t_{Q}}$
- **(C)** ${ }^{t_{P}=t_{Q}}$
- **(D)** $t_{P}>1.25 t_{Q}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) ${ }^{t_{P} < t_{Q}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Horizontal displacement of Q is more than P.
$X_Q > X_P$
Horizontal component of velocity is same
So $t_p = \dfrac{x_p}{v}$
$t_Q = \dfrac{x_Q}{v}$
$t_Q > t_p$

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A small bob $A$ of mass $m$ is attached to a massless rigid rod of length 1 m pivoted at point $P$ and kept at an angle of $60^{\circ}$ with vertical as shown in figure. At distance of 1 m below point $P$, an identical bob $B$ is kept at rest on a smooth horizontal surface that extends to a circular track of radius $R$ as shown in figure. If bob $B$ just manages to complete the circular path of radius $R$ upto a point $Q$ after being hit elastically by $\operatorname{bob} A$, then radius $R$ is $\_\_\_\_$ m.
 <img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/23S1_q_33_1.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{5}$
- **(B)** $\frac{2-\sqrt{3}}{5}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{5}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2+\sqrt{3}}{5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Bob A swings from $60°$ to vertical. Height drop $h = l(1-\cos 60°) = 1 \times 0.5 = 0.5$ m.
Velocity of A at bottom: $v_A = \sqrt{2gh} = \sqrt{2 \times 10 \times 0.5} = \sqrt{10}$ m/s.
Elastic collision between equal masses: B gets $v_B = \sqrt{10}$ m/s, A stops.
For B to just complete circular loop: $v_B^2 = 5gR$.
$10 = 5 \times 10 \times R \Rightarrow R = \frac{1}{5}$ m.

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements : 
 <b>Statement I</b>: An object moves from position $r_{1}$ to position $r_{2}$ under a conservative force field $\vec{F}$. The work done by the force is $W=-\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}} \vec{F} \cdot \overrightarrow{d r}$. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: Any object moving from one location to another location can follow infinite number of paths. Therefore, the amount of work done by the object changes with the path it follows for a conservative force. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I:
The work done by a force $\vec{F}$ during a displacement from $\vec{r}_1$ to $\vec{r}_2$ is defined as $W = \int_{r_1}^{r_2} \vec{F} \cdot d\vec{r}$.
The expression given in the statement, $W = -\int_{r_1}^{r_2} \vec{F} \cdot d\vec{r}$, is the definition of the change in potential energy ($\Delta U = -W$), not the work done by the force itself.
Thus, Statement I is false.

Statement II:
By definition, a conservative force is one for which the work done in moving an object between two points is independent of the path taken.
It only depends on the initial and final positions.
Therefore, the claim that work done changes with the path for a conservative force is false.

Since both statements are false, the correct option is (1).

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A body of mass 2 kg is moving along $x$-direction such that its displacement as function of time is given by $x(t)=\alpha t^{2}+\beta t+\gamma \mathrm{m}$, where $\alpha=1 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}^{2}, \beta=1 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$ and $\gamma=1 \mathrm{~m}$. The work done on the body during the time interval $t=2 \mathrm{~s}$ to $t=3 \mathrm{~s}$, is $\_\_\_\_$ J.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 12
- **(B)** 49
- **(C)** 42
- **(D)** 24

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 24**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $x(t) = t^2 + t + 1$, with $m = 2$ kg, find work from $t = 2$ s to $t = 3$ s.

Velocity: $v(t) = \frac{dx}{dt} = 2t + 1$
At $t = 2$ s: $v_i = 2(2) + 1 = 5$ m/s
At $t = 3$ s: $v_f = 2(3) + 1 = 7$ m/s

Using the work-energy theorem:
$W = \Delta KE = \frac{1}{2}m(v_f^2 - v_i^2) = \frac{1}{2}(2)(7^2 - 5^2) = 1(49 - 25) = 24$ J

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Potential energy ($V$) versus distance ($x$) is given by the graph. Rank various regions as per the magnitudes of the force (F) acting on a particle from high to low. 
 <img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S1_q_43_1.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $F_{C D}>F_{A B}>F_{B C}>F_{D E}$
- **(B)** $F_{C D}>F_{D E}>F_{A B}>F_{B C}$
- **(C)** $F_{B C}>F_{C D}>F_{D E}>F_{A B}$
- **(D)** $F_{B C}>F_{A B}>F_{D E}>F_{C D}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $F_{B C}>F_{A B}>F_{D E}>F_{C D}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Force is related to potential energy by $F = -\frac{dV}{dx}$.
Magnitude of force $|F| = \left|\frac{dV}{dx}\right|$ = |slope of V-x graph|
From graph coordinates: A(1, 0), B(1.5, 1), C(2, 4), D(4, 4), E(5, 3)
$|F_{AB}| = \frac{1-0}{1.5-1} = 2$
$|F_{BC}| = \frac{4-1}{2-1.5} = 6$
$|F_{CD}| = \frac{4-4}{4-2} = 0$
$|F_{DE}| = \frac{|3-4|}{5-4} = 1$
Ranking: $F_{BC} > F_{AB} > F_{DE} > F_{CD}$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2025 (8 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A block of mass 2 kg is attached to one end of a massless spring whose other end is fixed at a wall. The spring-mass system moves on a frictionless horizontal table. The spring's natural length is 2 m and spring constant is $200 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$. The block is pushed such that the length of the spring becomes 1 m and then released. At distance $\mathrm{x} \mathrm{m}(\mathrm{x} \lt 2)$ from the wall. the speed of the block will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $10[1-(2-x)]^{3 / 2} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(B)** $10\left[1-(2-x)^2\right]^{1 / 2} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(C)** $10\left[1-(2-\mathrm{x})^2\right] \mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(D)** $10\left[1-(2-\mathrm{x})^2\right]^2 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $10\left[1-(2-x)^2\right]^{1 / 2} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/DgF97cahn-QGmQmFL98z3dGmvq-7mYzIIGuGtfMNSsY.original.fullsize.png"/>
Given, Natural length of spring $=2 \mathrm{~m}$
Initial compression in spring $\left(\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{i}}\right)=1 \mathrm{~m}$
Final compression in spring $\left(\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{f}}\right)=(2-\mathrm{x}) \mathrm{m}$
Using energy conservation
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}}+\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}+\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{f}} \\
& 0+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Kx}_{\mathrm{i}}^2=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mv}^2+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Kx}_{\mathrm{f}}^2 \\
& \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mv}^2=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~K}\left(\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{i}}^2-\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{f}}^2\right) \\
& \frac{1}{2} \times 2 \times \mathrm{v}^2=\frac{1}{2} \times 200 \times\left(1^2-(2-\mathrm{x})^2\right) \\
& \mathrm{v}^2=100\left[1-(2-\mathrm{x})^2\right] \\
& \mathrm{v}=10\left[1-(2-\mathrm{x})^2\right]^{1 / 2}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which one of the following forces cannot be expressed in terms of potential energy?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Coulomb's force
- **(B)** Gravitational force
- **(C)** Frictional force
- **(D)** Restoring force

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Frictional force**

**Detailed Solution:**

Potential energy is defined for conservative force only. It is not defined for non-conservative force i.e. frictional force.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A block of mass 1 kg , moving along x with speed $\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{i}}=10 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$ enters a rough region ranging from $\mathrm{x}=0.1 \mathrm{~m}$ to $\mathrm{x}=1.9 \mathrm{~m}$. The retarding force acting on the block in this range is $\mathrm{F}_{\mathrm{r}}=-\mathrm{kx} \mathrm{N}$, with $\mathrm{k}=10 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$. Then the final speed of the block as it crosses rough region is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $10 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(B)** $4 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(C)** $6 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(D)** $8 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $8 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & a=\frac{F}{m}=-10 x \\ & v \frac{d v}{d x}=-10 x\end{aligned}$
$\int_{10}^{\mathrm{v}} \mathrm{vdv}=-10 \int_{0.1}^{1.9} \mathrm{xdx}$
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{v^2-100}{2}=-10\left(\frac{1.9^2-0.1}{2}\right)^2 \\ & v=8 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A piston of mass $M$ is hung from a massless spring whose restoring force law goes as $\mathrm{F}=-\mathrm{kx}^3$, where k is the spring constant of appropriate dimension. The piston separates the vertical chamber into two parts, where the bottom part is filled with ' $n$ ' moles of an ideal gas. An external work is done on the gas isothermally (at a constant temperature T) with the help of a heating filament (with negligible volume) mounted in lower part of the chamber, so that the piston goes up from a height $\mathrm{L}_0$ to $\mathrm{L}_1$, the total energy delivered by the filament is (Assume spring to be in its natural length before heating)
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ksdRTwfv9szYrgnBmJCJfNlCg9iElkR6joDrpw9pOpk.original.fullsize.png"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 n R T \ln \left(\frac{L_1}{L_0}\right)+2 \mathrm{Mg}\left(L_1-L_0\right)+\frac{k}{3}\left(L_1^3-L_0^3\right)$
- **(B)** $n R T \ln \left(\frac{L_1^2}{L_0^2}\right)+\frac{M g}{2}\left(L_1-L_0\right)+\frac{k}{4}\left(L_1^4-L_0^4\right)$
- **(C)** $n R T \ln \left(\frac{L_1}{L_0}\right)+M g\left(L_1-L_0\right)+\frac{k}{4}\left(L_1^4-L_0^4\right)$
- **(D)** $n R T \ln \left(\frac{L_1}{L_0}\right)+M g\left(L_1-L_0\right)+\frac{3 k}{4}\left(L_1^4-L_0^4\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $n R T \ln \left(\frac{L_1}{L_0}\right)+M g\left(L_1-L_0\right)+\frac{k}{4}\left(L_1^4-L_0^4\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using WET
Total energy supplied $=$ gravitational potential energy + spring potential energy + work done by gas
$\begin{aligned}
& \operatorname{Mg} \quad\left(L_1-L_0\right)+\int_{L_0}^{L_1} k^3 d x+n R T \ell n \\
& {\left[\frac{L_1 \mathrm{~A}}{\mathrm{~L}_0 \mathrm{~A}}\right]+\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{ext}}=0} \\
& \frac{\mathrm{~K}}{4}\left[\mathrm{x}^4\right]_{\mathrm{L}_0}^{\mathrm{L}_1}+\operatorname{Mg}\left(\mathrm{L}_1-\mathrm{L}_0\right)+\int_{\mathrm{L}_0}^{\mathrm{L}_1} k x^3 d x+n R T \ell n
\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\frac{L_1}{L_0}\right]+\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{ext}}=0} \\ & \frac{\mathrm{k}}{4}\left(\mathrm{~L}_1^4-\mathrm{L}_0^4\right)+\operatorname{Mg} \quad\left(\mathrm{L}_1-\mathrm{L}_0\right)+\mathrm{nRT} \ell \mathrm{n} \\ & {\left[\frac{\mathrm{L}_1}{\mathrm{~L}_0}\right]+\mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{ext}}=0} \\ & \mathrm{~W}_{\mathrm{ext}}=\frac{\mathrm{k}}{4}\left(\mathrm{~L}_1^4-\mathrm{L}_0^4\right)+\operatorname{Mg}\left(\mathrm{L}_1-\mathrm{L}_0\right)+\mathrm{nRT} \ell \mathrm{n}\left[\frac{\mathrm{L}_1}{\mathrm{~L}_0}\right]\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is released from height $S$ above the surface of the earth. At certain height its kinetic energy is three times its potential energy. The height from the surface of the earth and the speed of the particle at that instant are respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\mathrm{S}}{2}, \sqrt{\frac{3 \mathrm{gS}}{2}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mathrm{S}}{2}, \frac{3 \mathrm{gS}}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{S}}{4}, \frac{3 \mathrm{gS}}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mathrm{S}}{4}, \sqrt{\frac{3 \mathrm{gS}}{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{\mathrm{S}}{4}, \sqrt{\frac{3 \mathrm{gS}}{2}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{V}^2=0+2 \mathrm{~g}(\mathrm{~S}-\mathrm{x}) \\
& \mathrm{V}^2=2 \mathrm{~g}(\mathrm{~S}-\mathrm{x})
\end{aligned}$
At B, Potential energy $=m g x$
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{mgx}=3 \times \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mv}^2 \\
& \mathrm{gx}=\frac{3}{2} \times 2 \mathrm{~g}(\mathrm{~S}-\mathrm{x}) \\
& 4 \mathrm{x}=\mathrm{S} \\
& \mathrm{x}=\frac{\mathrm{S}}{4}
\end{aligned}$
$\Rightarrow \mathrm{V}=\sqrt{2 \mathrm{~g} \times \frac{3 \mathrm{~S}}{4}}=\sqrt{\frac{3 \mathrm{gS}}{2}}$

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A bead of mass ' $m$ ' slides without friction on the wall of a vertical circular hoop of radius ' $R$ ' as shown in figure. The bead moves under the combined action of gravity and a massless spring ( k ) attached to the bottom of the hoop. The equilibrium length of the spring is ' $R$ '. If the bead is released from top of the hoop with (negligible) zero initial speed, velocity of bead, when the length of spring becomes ' R ', would be (spring constant is ' k ', g is accleration due to gravity)
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5iEIyOx5hQy7vc5_gODOCC1Q_pX5FEYvspM2Urdpdkc.original.fullsize.png"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{3 \mathrm{Rg}+\frac{\mathrm{kR}^2}{\mathrm{~m}}}$
- **(B)** $2 \sqrt{\mathrm{gR}+\frac{\mathrm{kR}^2}{\mathrm{~m}}}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{2 \mathrm{Rg}+\frac{\mathrm{kR}^2}{\mathrm{~m}}}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{2 \mathrm{Rg}+\frac{4 \mathrm{kR}^2}{\mathrm{~m}}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{3 \mathrm{Rg}+\frac{\mathrm{kR}^2}{\mathrm{~m}}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dOsEa7kZZUn6f2qFXuY3Sf20ICoBd1Fp3p4eVVqYi6I.original.fullsize.png">

$\begin{aligned} \text { Work done by gravity } & =m g\left(2 R-R \cos 60^{\circ}\right) \\ & =\frac{3 m g R}{2}\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} \text { Work done by spring } & =-\frac{1}{2} k\left(0^2-R^2\right) \\ & =\frac{1}{2} k R^2\end{aligned}$
Net work = change in kinetic energy
i.e. $\frac{3 m g R}{2}+\frac{k R^2}{2}=\frac{1}{2} m v^2$
or $\quad v^2=3 g R+\frac{k R^2}{m}$
or $\quad v=\sqrt{3 g R+\frac{k R^2}{m}}$

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Given below are two statements: one is labelled as Assertion $\mathbf{A}$ and the other is labelled as Reason $\mathbf{R}$
Assertion A: In a central force field, the work done is independent of the path chosen.
Reason R: Every force encountered in mechanics does not have an associated potential energy.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathbf{A}$ is false but $\mathbf{R}$ is true
- **(B)** Both $\mathbf{A}$ and $\mathbf{R}$ are true but $\mathbf{R}$ is NOT the correct explanation of $\mathbf{A}$
- **(C)** $\mathbf{A}$ is true but $\mathbf{R}$ is false
- **(D)** Both $\mathbf{A}$ and $\mathbf{R}$ are true and $\mathbf{R}$ is the correct explanation of $\mathbf{A}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Both $\mathbf{A}$ and $\mathbf{R}$ are true but $\mathbf{R}$ is NOT the correct explanation of $\mathbf{A}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Assertion is correct as central forces are conservative in nature, i.e. work done is independent of path.

Reason is true as some forces in mechanics like, friction are non-conservative because work done depends on path and only conservative forces have an associated potential energy.
Also, reason does not explain assertion.

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A ball having kinetic energy KE, is projected at an angle of $60^{\circ}$ from the horizontal. What will be the kinetic energy of ball at the highest point of its flight ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{(\mathrm{KE})}{8}$
- **(B)** $\frac{(\mathrm{KE})}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{(\mathrm{KE})}{16}$
- **(D)** $\frac{(\mathrm{KE})}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\frac{(\mathrm{KE})}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initial K.E,
$\text { K.E. }=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mu}^2$

Speed at heighest point
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{V}=\mathrm{u} \cos 60^{\circ}=\frac{\mathrm{u}}{2} \\
& \therefore \mathrm{KE}_2=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}\left(\frac{\mathrm{u}}{2}\right)^2 \\
& =\frac{1}{4} \times \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mu}^2 \\
& =\frac{\mathrm{KE}}{4}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q18. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A stationary particle breaks into two parts of masses $m_A$ and $m_B$ which move with velocities $v_A$ and $v_B$ respectively. The ratio of their kinetic energies $\left(K_B: K_A\right)$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $v_B: v_A$
- **(B)** $m_B: m_A$
- **(C)** $m_B v_B: m_A v_A$
- **(D)** $1: 1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $v_B: v_A$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initial momentum is zero.
$\begin{aligned} & \text { Hence }\left|\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{A}}\right|=\left|\mathrm{P}_{\mathrm{B}}\right| \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{A}} \mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}}=\mathrm{m}_{\mathrm{B}} \mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}} \\ & \frac{(\mathrm{KE})_{\mathrm{A}}}{(\mathrm{KE})_{\mathrm{B}}}=\frac{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}_{\mathrm{A}} \mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{A}}^2}{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}_{\mathrm{B}} \mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}}^2}=\frac{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{A}}}{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}}} \\ & \frac{(\mathrm{KE})_{\mathrm{B}}}{(\mathrm{KE})_{\mathrm{A}}}=\frac{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}}}{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{A}}}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q19. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A bullet of mass $50 \mathrm{~g}$ is fired with a speed $100 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$ on a plywood and emerges with $40 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$. The percentage loss of kinetic energy is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $84 \%$
- **(B)** $16 \%$
- **(C)** $32 \%$
- **(D)** $44 \%$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $84 \%$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}} & =\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}(100)^2 \\ \mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}} & =\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}(40)^2 \\ \% \text { loss } & =\frac{\left|\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}-\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}}\right|}{\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}}} \times 100 \\ & =\frac{\left|\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}(40)^2-\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}(100)^2\right|}{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}(100)^2} \times 100 \\ & =\frac{|1600-100 \times 100|}{100}=84 \%\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q20. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A body of mass $50 \mathrm{~kg}$ is lifted to a height of $20 \mathrm{~m}$ from the ground in the two different ways as shown in the figures. The ratio of work done against the gravity in both the respective cases, will be :
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/S-uVz1Dhc1SH2VTEV9EBncHPilM6gwiUmqP_AgakPLk.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1: 2$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{3}: 2$
- **(C)** $2: 1$
- **(D)** $1: 1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1: 1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done by gravity is independent of path. It depends only on vertical displacement so work done in both cases will be same.

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A body of $m \mathrm{~kg}$ slides from rest along the curve of vertical circle from point $A$ to $B$ in friction less path. The velocity of the body at $B$ is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ogI6ALu6CRH_S1yuxN8XzJeJuZkm57N_XSBdZk0nDQ8.original.fullsize.png">
$\text { (given, } R=14 \mathrm{~m}, g=10 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}^2 \text { and } \sqrt{2}=1.4 \text { ) }$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $16.7 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(B)** $19.8 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(C)** $10.6 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(D)** $21.9 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $21.9 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/FOiBkF3qGbB4VWxKLLBLnKqgk9RHfRoCeg1Vdj4R-yQ.original.fullsize.png">
Apply W.E.T. from A to B
$\begin{aligned}
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{W}_{\mathrm{mg}}=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{B}}-\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{A}} \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{mg} \times\left(\frac{\mathrm{R}}{\sqrt{2}}+\mathrm{R}\right)=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mv}_{\mathrm{B}}^2-0\left\{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{A}}=0 \text { rest }\right\} \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{mgR} \frac{(\sqrt{2}+1)}{\sqrt{2}}=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mv}_{\mathrm{B}}^2 \\
& \Rightarrow \sqrt{\mathrm{gR} \frac{2(\sqrt{2}+1)}{\sqrt{2}}}=\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}} \\
& \Rightarrow \sqrt{\frac{10 \times 14 \times 2(2.4)}{1.4}}=\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}} \\
& \Rightarrow 21.9=\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{B}}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q22. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If a rubber ball falls from a height $h$ and rebounds upto the height of $h / 2$. The percentage loss of total energy of the initial system as well as velocity ball before it strikes the ground, respectively, are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $50 \%, \sqrt{2 \mathrm{gh}}$
- **(B)** $50 \%, \sqrt{\mathrm{gh}}$
- **(C)** $40 \%, \sqrt{2 \mathrm{gh}}$
- **(D)** $50 \%, \sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{gh}}{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $50 \%, \sqrt{2 \mathrm{gh}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \text { Velocity just before collision }=\sqrt{2 \mathrm{gh}} \\
& \text { Velocity just after collision }=\sqrt{2 \mathrm{~g}\left(\frac{\mathrm{h}}{2}\right)} \\
& \therefore \Delta \mathrm{KE}=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~m}(2 \mathrm{gh})-\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mgh} \\
& =\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mgh} \\
& \therefore \% \text { loss in energy } \\
& =\frac{\Delta \mathrm{KE}}{\mathrm{KE}_{\mathrm{i}}} \times 100=\frac{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mgh}}{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{mg} 2 \mathrm{~h}} \times 100=50 \%
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q23. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A solid circular disc of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> rolls along a horizontal floor so that its center of mass has a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The absolute value of work done on the disc to stop it is ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using work-energy theorem, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>∆</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>I</mi><msup><mi>ω</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><msup><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Absolute work <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, required value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q24. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An artillery piece of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> fires a shell of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> horizontally. Instantaneously after the firing, the ratio of kinetic energy of the artillery and that of the shell is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As linear momentum will be conserved, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mover><mi>p</mi><mo>⇀</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mover><mi>p</mi><mo>⇀</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced></math></p><p>Now, kinetic energy can be written as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Also <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> is the same for both masses. We can write,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>∝</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A particle is placed at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> of a frictionless track <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABC</mi></math> as shown in figure. It is gently pushed towards right. The speed of the particle when it reaches the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo></math>Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open=""><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b179c38-6291-497f-abe6-f036b3493e88.png" style="width: 400px; height: 147px;"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b179c38-6291-497f-abe6-f036b3493e88.png" style="width: 400px; height: 147px;"/></p><p>Applying conservation of mechanical energy between point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>(1)=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A bob of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is suspended by a light string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>. It is imparted a minimum horizontal velocity at the lowest point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> such that it just completes half circle reaching the top most position <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>. The ratio of kinetic energies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>. </mo><msub><mo>)</mo><mi>A</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>. </mo><msub><mo>)</mo><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac></math> is :</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/70faeffc-7541-4ec4-b7b9-e636d94cd0d0.png" style="width: 250px; height: 263px;"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The velocity given is minimum, just enough to complete verticle circle. At the top most point, tension of the string will be zero and gravitational force will provide the required centripetal force.</p><p>Therefore, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>H</mi></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>L</mi></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>H</mi></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></math></p><p>Apply energy conservation between point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mi>H</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi>L</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>⇒</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math></p><p>Also, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>V</mi><mi>H</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt></math></p><p>Required ratio, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mi>A</mi></msub></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>L</mi></msqrt><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The bob of a pendulum was released from a horizontal position. The length of the pendulum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. If it dissipates <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of its initial energy against air resistance, the speed with which the bob arrives at the lowest point is: [Use, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/bba87c54-8494-49ca-97ff-5b33d791769a.png" style="width: 163px; height: 170px;"/></p><p>Given the length of the pendulum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ℓ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>.</p><p>The initial potential energy of the pendulum at the horizontal position is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>ℓ</mi><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>The final kinetic energy of the pendulum at the lowermost position is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>So, from equations (1) and (2), it follows that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>ℓ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>180</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>180</mn></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The potential energy function (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> ) of a particle in a region of space is given as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>. Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> are in meter. The magnitude of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> - component of force (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> ) acting on the particle at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi></math></p><p>The expression for the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo></math>component of the force can be calculated as follows: </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>x</mi></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>∂</mo><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>∂</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Hence, at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>, the value of the force is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi>x</mi></msub></mfenced></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q29. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two bodies of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> are moving with equal kinetic energies. The ratio of magnitude of their linear momentum is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>K</mi></mfenced></math> of an object is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi>K</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Following equation (1), for the two given bodies, it can be written that</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msubsup><mi>p</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo> </mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>25</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2023 (15 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A body is released from a height equal to the radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>R</mi></mfenced></math> of the earth. The velocity of the body when it strikes the surface of the earth will be: (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo></math> acceleration due to gravity on the earth.)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Using conservation of mechanical energy,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mi>R</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mi>GM</mi><mi>R</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi></msqrt></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q31. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A car accelerates from rest of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>u</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The energy spent in this process is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. The energy required to accelerate the car from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>u</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>u</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> is _____.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The kinetic energy of a moving particle is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>When the final velocity of the particle becomes twice the initial velocity, the kinetic energy of the particle can be written as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi>n</mi><mi>E</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>u</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Divide equation (2) by equation (1) and solve to calculate the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><mi>E</mi></mfrac></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q32. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is moving with a velocity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> Its kinetic energy will be</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>140</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>140</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>140</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The kinetic energy is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Differentiating the above equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>(</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Dividing by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>K</mi></mrow><mi>K</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mi>v</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>K</mi></mrow><mn>1000</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>20</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>140</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>140</mn><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A closed circular tube of average radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>, whose inner walls are rough, is kept in vertical plane. A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> just fit inside the tube. The speed of block is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, when it is introduced at the top of tube. After completing five oscillations, the block stops at the bottom region of tube. The work done by the tube on the block is ________ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. (Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>).</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/237a2764-b00c-41e6-8418-81cdde6fcd56.png" style="width: 250px; height: 201px;" /></p><p> </p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **245**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The data given is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Total work done by the tube on the block is equal to loss in mechanical energy, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>22</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>245</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

close="]" open="["><mrow><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>The formula to calculate the potential energy acquired by the ball when it attains the maximum height is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Equate <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi>h</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p></blockquote>

### Q35. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is moving with a momentum of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Now a force of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> acts on the body in the direction of its motion for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>. The increase in the Kinetic energy of the body is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **30**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The formula to calculate the change on momentum of the object is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8747;</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Substitute the values of the known parameters into equation (1) and solve to calculate the final momentum of the object.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8658;</mo></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>The formula to calculate increase in kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> of the particle is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msup><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><msub><mi>p</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Substitute the values of the known parameters into equation (2) to calculate the required increase in kinetic energy.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msup><mn>20</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>kg</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q36. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A bullet of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> moving horizontally with speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> hits a wooden block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> kept on a horizontal rough surface. The bullet gets embedded into the block and moves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> before coming to rest. The coefficient of friction between the block and the surface is _______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>90</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>50</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>65</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Let the mass of the bullet be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> and mass of the block be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>. It is given that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. By conservation of momentum,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>v</mi></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>400</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>)</mo><mi>v</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>The force acting on the block </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mi>g</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p>Using  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><msup><mo>&#39;</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>S</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The momentum of a body is increased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math>. The percentage increase in the kinetic energy of the body is_<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>%</mo><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The relation of kinetic energy and momentum is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>The new value of momentum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>p</mi></math></p><p>The new value of kinetic energy is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>K</mi></math></p><p>Hence, the percentage increase in kinetic energy is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">K</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#39;</mo><mo mathvariant="italic">-</mo><mi mathvariant="italic">K</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">K</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>K</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow><mi>K</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A body is dropped on ground from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>h</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and after hitting the ground, it rebounds to a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. If the ratio of velocities of the body just before and after hitting ground is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>, then percentage loss in kinetic energy of the body is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is _____.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **375**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The given ratio of the velocities is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>The percentage loss in kinetic energy is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mi>m</mi><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></msub></mstyle><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>400</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mi>x</mi></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>400</mn></mstyle></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mi>x</mi></mstyle><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mn>400</mn></mstyle></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>375</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A small particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> moves in such a way that its potential energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi></math> is constant and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> is the distance of the particle from origin. Assuming Bohr&rsquo;s quantization of momentum and circular orbit, the radius of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>th</mi></msup></math> orbit will be proportional to</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The data given is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>From Bohr&#39;s quantization, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>In an orbit the value of the kinetic energy is half of the potential energy,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Substituting the value of equation (i) in the kinetic energy,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msqrt><mi>n</mi></msqrt></math></p></blockquote>

</details>
