### Q40. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A block is fastened to a horizontal spring. The block is pulled to a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> from its equilibrium position (at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>) on a frictionless surface from rest. The energy of the block at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. The spring constant of the spring is ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **67**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6f059c9c-3beb-4518-8627-7ec4bd591b02-9896819.PNG" style="width: 235px; height: 100px;" /></p><p>At first the block is pulled to a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>(extreme position).</p><p>Potential energy of block is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math> and kinetic energy os block, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>Now, when the block is at distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c64ff713-c5d3-4c25-9f37-a95e645b6f16-9896819_1.PNG" style="width: 258px; height: 85px;" /></p><p>Potential energy is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>U</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mfenced><mfrac><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and kinetic energy is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Total energy of block will be conserved, so </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mfrac><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msubsup><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>200</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>67</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A lift of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is descending with speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Its supporting cable begins to slip thus allowing it to fall with a constant acceleration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The kinetic energy of the lift at the end of fall through to a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> will be ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>kJ</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>Using kinematics third equation of motion, final speed of lift is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mfenced><mn>6</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Now, kinetic energy of the lift is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>)</mo><mn>28</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>7000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> mass takes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> to reach ground when dropped from a certain height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> above surface of earth. The loss of potential energy in the last second of fall is ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>. [Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **300**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Displacement in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi>th</mi></msup></math> second is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>n</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Here, displacement is 8<sup>th</sup> second is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p>Loss of potential energy is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>8</mn></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>75</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>An object of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> initially at rest on a smooth horizontal plane starts moving under the action of force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>. In the process of its linear motion, the angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi></math> (as shown in figure) between the direction of force and horizontal varies as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math> is a constant and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is the distance covered by the object from its initial position. The expression of kinetic energy of the object will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">k</mi></mfrac><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> is ______.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/01b39f2a-6cbf-4008-af64-51e019c28c14-9894258.png" style="width: 300px; height: 157px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7fad4c6d-9244-4afa-8474-0022847ca6bc-9894258_2.png" style="width: 250px; height: 259px;" /></p><p>As seen in the free body diagram of the object, normal force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math>, weight <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math> and force in parallel and perpendicular directions are acting.</p><p>Using Newton&#39;s law of motion</p><p>Along horizontal direction, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>ma</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Integrating the above relation, we have</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>v</mi></msubsup><mi>v</mi><mo>d</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi>x</mi></msubsup><mi>cos</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>d</mo><mi>x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mfrac><mi>sin</mi><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></math></p><p>Thus, kinetic energy of the object will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi></mfrac><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p>Thus, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A spherical body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> starting from rest acquires a kinetic energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> at the end of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>5</mn><mtext>th&#160;</mtext></msup></math> second. The force acted on the body is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **40**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given kinetic energy, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>10000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>10000</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>10000</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Also,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>at</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Therefore, force acting</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If momentum of a body is increased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>%</mo></math>, then its kinetic energy increases by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>36</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>44</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>48</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>44</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>A body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math>, moving with momentum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>p</mi></math> and kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi></math> are related as follows,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.
As the momentum of the body is increased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>%</mo></math>, then its momentum becomes,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>p</mi></math>
and the mass of the body remains unchanged. So, kinetic energy is directly related to the square of momentum. </p><p>Hence, new kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msup><mi>K</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup></mfenced></math> will be,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>K</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>K</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>.</p><p>So, the percentage change in kinetic energy
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>K</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow><mi>K</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></math> 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn><mi>K</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow><mi>K</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>44</mn><mo>%</mo></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q46. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A bullet of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> having initial kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> is shot inside a long swimming pool as shown in the figure. If it&#39;s kinetic energy reduces to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> within <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>, the minimum length of the pool, the bullet has to travel so that it completely comes to rest is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/186e6a82-a8e4-44f3-aade-46cde3e0c363-image.png" style="width: 160px; height: 89px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>125</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Let us assume that the motion of the bullet is of constant retardation inside the water.</p><p>Initial kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Let the retardation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math>, then after <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>30</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Stopping distance for the constant retardation is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mn>30</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>20</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>.</p><p>Therefore, the minimum length of the pool required is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A block of mass &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math>&#39; (as shown in figure) moving with kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> compresses a spring through a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> when, its speed is halved. The value of spring constant of used spring will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo></math>_____.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f9ed0b74-c6e4-4159-a921-4b9b3ba1163a-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 99px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **24**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Using work <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo></math> energy theorem</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mrow><mi>net</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>K</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>K</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>E</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>E</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>K</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn><mi>E</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Thus, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Two inclined planes are placed as shown in figure.
A block is projected from the Point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> of inclined plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> along its surface with a velocity just sufficient to carry it to the top Point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> at a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. After reaching the Point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> the block slides down on inclined plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math>. Time it takes to reach to the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> from point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mfenced><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>. The value of t is _____ (use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7870fb78-705f-4e0b-9ad4-1cd73c39b00c-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 134px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a47cccb1-8680-480f-9dc1-e4f870aab86d-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 143px;" /></p><p>From energy conservation at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> </p><p>At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Acceleration of a particle moving on a smooth incline is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math>. Therefore, along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>g</mi><mi>sin</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p>Using equation of motion,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p></blockquote><blockquote><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>C</mi></math></p><p>Using second equation of motion, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>u</mi><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>a</mi><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>sin</mi><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfenced><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p><p>So total time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>As per the given figure, two blocks each of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> are connected to a spring of spring constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. If both are given velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math> in opposite directions, then maximum elongation of the spring is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9be38da4-5bff-4329-8ffc-413921cd5f49-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 47px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>v</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Let the maximum elongation of the spring be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f546ad99-8341-4ce6-9786-757e1675224c-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 215px;" /></p><p>Using energy conservation</p><p>Loss in kinetic energy of both blocks<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math>Gain in spring energy</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></mrow></mfenced><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A bag of sand of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is suspended by a rope. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> bullet of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> travelling with speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> gets embedded in it, then loss of kinetic energy will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>19</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>If the final velocity of the bullet and the bag is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>, applying conservation of momentum,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>v</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>v</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Loss in kinetic energy will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>In the given figure, the block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is dropped from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math>. The expression for kinetic energy of block when it reaches point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#39;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></math> is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8f913b10-c535-4741-9267-58d87bcd1941-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 220px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><msubsup><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Here, gain in kinetic energy<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math>loss in potential energy</p><p>Now loss in potential energy of particle in falling from height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Thus, kinetic energy at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Water falls from a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> high dam at the rate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> per hour. Fifty percentage of gravitational potential energy can be converted into electrical energy. Using this hydro electric energy number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi></math> lamps, that can be lit, is
(Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math> efficiency is given, half of the energy of the falling water will be used by the bulbs. Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow><mn>3600</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A pendulum is suspended by a string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. The mass of the bob of the pendulum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>. The bob is pulled aside until the string is at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> with vertical as shown in the figure. After releasing the bob, the maximum velocity attained by the bob will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. (if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/15cf4e56-b6fb-474c-a931-ef986232d50f-1.jpg" style="width: 180px; height: 158px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a78ba12c-bea2-4d59-9574-c54298d2d263-a39e9882-fc46-497f-813c-3a19651a849a-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 164px;" /></p><p>Velocity will be maximum at the bottom point when potential energy of the pendulum will be zero(considering bottom point as reference for potential energy).</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>l</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>l</mi><mi>cos</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>l</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>    </p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A rolling wheel of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is on an inclined plane at position <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and connected to a mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> through a string of fixed length and pulley as shown in figure.
Consider <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>R</mi></math> as friction free surface.
The velocity of centre of mass of the wheel when it reaches at the bottom <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> of the inclined plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mi>x</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> (rounded off to the nearest integer) is _____.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1b30fe73-572a-46e1-9e41-da0e11a001c5-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 124px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>If we take the wheel as a ring then its moment of inertia will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>Using mechanical energy conservation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>M</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>I</mi><msup><mi>&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>12</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>12</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></msqrt></math></p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> block moving at a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>ms</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> compresses a spring through a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> when its speed is halved. The spring constant of the spring will be _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Nm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **600**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given: Mass of the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>, initial velocity of the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, and final velocity of the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p>As non-conservative forces are not acting on the system we can conserve energy.</p><p>Thus, loss in kinetic energy of block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math> Gain in potential energy of spring</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>12</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>6</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Potential energy as a function of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>A</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>B</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> is the interatomic distance, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> are positive constants. The equilibrium distance between the two atoms will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>B</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>B</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>B</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>A</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>B</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Now, for equilibrium, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>So, differentiating the given equation,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>11</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>11</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>B</mi></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>A</mi></mrow><mi>B</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A ball of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> is dropped from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> on a platform fixed at the top of a vertical spring (as shown in figure). The ball stays on the platform and the platform is depressed by a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>. The spring constant is _____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
(Use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/770eafa4-cf51-4b4b-9514-2b1c8d8c9864-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 231px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **120**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/edf8848b-dc71-4b91-beba-68ad086e439d-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 219px;" /></p><p>Loss in the gravitational potential energy of the ball= gain in the potential energy by the spring</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>P</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>gravitational</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>P</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>spring</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>h</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mi>h</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>120</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A particle experiences a variable force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math> in a horizontal <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi></math> plane. Assume distance in meters and force is newton. If the particle moves from point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> to point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi></math> plane, then Kinetic Energy changes by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using Work energy theorem,</p><p>Kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>d</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></msubsup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>d</mo><mi>y</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>19</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn></math> Joules</p>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

An engine is attached to a wagon through a shock absorber of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The system with a total mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is moving with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>72</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> when the brakes are applied to bring it to rest. In the process of the system being brought to rest, the spring of the shock absorber gets compressed by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>90</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of energy of the wagon is lost due to friction, the spring constant is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **16**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Work <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mtext>triction&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mtext>spring&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>90</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mtext>spring&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>w</mi><mtext>spring&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>20</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>40000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>20</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q60. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> dropped from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> reaches the ground with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></msqrt><mo>.</mo></math> The value of work done by the air-friction is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>68</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>64</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>64</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>68</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi>W</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>:</mo></math> work done against friction
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>64</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>f</mi></msub></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0.68</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>
Work done by friction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>&#39;</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.68</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q61. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A block moving horizontally on a smooth surface with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> splits into two parts with masses in the ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></math> If the smaller part moves at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> in the same direction, then the fractional change in kinetic energy is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d1949edf-a56b-49ae-8adf-fb165eda8871-image.png" style="width: 596px; height: 58px;" />
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>60</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>120</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>60</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mtext>m&#160;s</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>40</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mi>m</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>30</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>60</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>{</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1800</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3600</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>}</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>5400</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn><mi>m</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>900</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>800</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>800</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A block moving horizontally on a smooth surface with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> splits into two equal parts. If one of the parts moves at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> in the same direction, then the fractional change in the kinetic energy will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo></math> ________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/efef3b84-8863-4679-9eac-3046b0e1563c-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 109px;" /><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>20</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>60</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>40</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>40</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3200</mn></math>
Fractional change <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The height of victoria&#39;s falls is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>63</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. What is the difference in the temperature of water at the top and at the bottom of the fall? [Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cal</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> and specific heat of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="" close="]"><mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cal</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mprescripts/><none/><mo>&#8728;</mo></mmultiscripts><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>76</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>476</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>147</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>014</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>147</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Here use the concept of conservation of energy, here potential energy is converted in to heat energy, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math>, here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>s</mi></math>, is the mass of the liquid, height of the fall, and specific heat capacity then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>63</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>147</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A uniform chain of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> overhangs a smooth table with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> laying on the table. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi></math> is the kinetic energy of the chain in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> as it completely slips off the table, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi></math> is
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mtext>Take&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **40**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0b6b6f3d-e2f9-48ab-916a-ba6a394fdbe9-0d77efc0-5fad-4554-a164-3eae6e10a7e5-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 208px;" /></p><p>Apply conservation of energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>KE</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>K.E.</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>K.E.</mtext><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>
