### Q65. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Given below is the plot of a potential energy function <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo></math> for a system, in which a particle is in one dimensional motion, while a conservative force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo></math> acts on it. Suppose that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>mech&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>, the incorrect statement for this system is :</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f7dd0df4-adcb-4dcd-81ed-0b0628b65d9b-image.png" style="width: 284px; height: 242px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>. is constant throughout the region.
- **(B)** at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>. is smallest and the particle is moving at the slowest speed.
- **(C)** at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>. is greatest and the particle is moving at the fastest speed.
- **(D)** at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></math>. is smallest and the particle is moving at the slowest speed.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(A) at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>mech</mi><mo>.&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>constant</mi></math></p><p>(B) at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo></math> constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>mech</mi><mo>.&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Particle is at rest.</p><p>(C) At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mrow><mi>mech</mi><mo>.&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KE</mi></math> is greatest, and particle is moving at fastest speed.</p><p>(D) At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the kinetic energy of a moving body becomes four times its initial kinetic energy, then the percentage change in its momentum will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>%</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mo>%</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>%</mo></math> change <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>%</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A ball of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>, moving with a velocity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, collides with a spring of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and force constant <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The length of the compressed spring is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>, to the nearest integer, is ___ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let&#39;s say the compression in the spring by:<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>. So, by work-energy theorem we have</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>v</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Final length of spring,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> moves in a circular orbit under the central potential field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mfenced><mi>r</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> is a positive constant. The correct radius - velocity graph of the particle&#39;s motion is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/be2e85fe-3ee4-4128-86f7-6846a1520a37-image.png" style="width: 160px; height: 131px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b354441-7a41-454a-9f38-65fdad946ec7-image.png" style="width: 162px; height: 126px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4233aae4-6de2-4e9d-9140-176dd023492a-image.png" style="width: 160px; height: 133px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/85cb04ae-97e9-4a9c-9b8e-366b136901d4-image.png" style="width: 162px; height: 132px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/be2e85fe-3ee4-4128-86f7-6846a1520a37-image.png" style="width: 160px; height: 131px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>C</mi><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>F</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>As shown in the figure, a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is placed at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>.</mo></math> When the particle is slightly displaced to its right, it starts moving and reaches the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The speed of the particle at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open=""><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> to the nearest integer is</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/296c83de-f840-439d-8a8d-91f8074e5fd8-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 149px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using work-energy theorem, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>g</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

</details>

### Q70. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A boy is rolling a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> ball on the frictionless floor with the speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> The ball gets deflected by an obstacle on the way. After deflection it moves with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of its initial kinetic energy. What is the speed of the ball now?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>19</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>41</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>u</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Initial kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>After deflection it moves with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math></p><p>Now, let the final speed be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>'</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>'</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> then:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>20</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>47</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Two particles having masses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> respectively are moving with equal kinetic energies. The ratio of the magnitudes of their linear momentum is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> will be ___.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msubsup><mi>p</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msubsup><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>p</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>p</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q72. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The potential energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>U</mi></mfenced></math> of a diatomic molecule is a function dependent on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> (interatomic distance) as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>10</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#946;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math> where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#946;</mi></math> are positive constants. The equilibrium distance between two atoms will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mi>&#946;</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi></mfrac></msup><mo>,</mo></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo></math>______ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For equilibrium</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>11</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>11</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mi>&#946;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mi>&#946;</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mi>&#946;</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></msup><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Two solids <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> respectively are moving with equal linear momentum. The ratio of their kinetic energies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></mfenced><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>:</mo><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo></mrow></mfenced><mi>B</mi></msub></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math> so the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> will be _________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Kinetic energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msub><mi>K</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>A</mi></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q74. JEE Main 2020 (06 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the potential energy between two molecules is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>A</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>B</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>12</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math> then at equilibrium, separation between molecules, and the potential energy are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow><mi>A</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></msup><mo>,</mo><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow><mi>A</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>A</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>B</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>12</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>For equilibrium</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow><mi>A</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p><p>Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q75. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Hydrogen ion and singly ionized helium atom are accelerated, from rest, through the same potential difference. The ratio of final speeds of hydrogen and helium ions is close to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>7</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>7</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>qV</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi>He</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A cricket ball of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is thrown vertically up by a bowling machine so that it rises to a maximum height of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> after leaving the machine. If the part pushing the ball applies a constant force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> on the ball applies a constant force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> on the ball and moves horizontally a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> while launching the ball, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **150**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>From work energy theorem <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>mg</mi><mfenced><mn>20</mn></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>mg</mi><mfrac><mfenced><mn>20</mn></mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>mg</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mn>200</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>200</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>150</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A particle <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> slides down a frictionless track <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> starting from rest at a point <math><mi>A</mi></math> (height <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> ). After reaching <math><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo><mi></mi></math> the particle continues to move freely in air as a projectile. When it reaching its highest point <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> (height <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> ), the kinetic energy of the particle (in <math><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> ) is: (Figure drawn is schematic and not to scale; take <math><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> ) ______________.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c6cc6ed3-484e-4624-8825-e784ddc737e6.png" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **10**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>KE</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>P</mtext></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mtext>P</mtext></msub><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mtext>A</mtext></msub></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A wedge of mass <math><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>m</mi></math> lies on a frictionless plane. A particle of mass <math><mi>m</mi></math> approaches the wedge with speed <math><mi>v</mi><mo>.</mo></math> There is no friction between the particle and the plane or between the particle and the wedge. The maximum height climbed by the particle on the wedge is given by:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From conservation of linear momentum, 
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f2eed996-960d-4254-8307-edae26bc7b82-image11.png"> 
<math><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub></math>  
<math><msub><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
From conservation of mechanical energy, 
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>  
<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>  
<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>  
<math><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q79. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle moves in one dimension from rest under the influence of a force that varies with the distance traveled by the particle as shown in the figure. The kinetic energy of the particle after it has traveled <math><mn>3</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1a3433be-8e6d-4787-8302-b4795b388292-image.png" style="width: 371px; height: 371px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>6.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>6.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4eade05e-b6f8-42af-8289-a7e3592ee152-image.png" style="width: 426px; height: 426px;" />
Change in K.E.<math><mo>=</mo></math>Work done<math><mo>=</mo></math>Area
Area<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>6.5</mn></math>
<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Change in kinetic energy<math><mo>=</mo><mn>6.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q80. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> is released with an initial velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> along the curve from the point <math><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo></math> as shown in the figure. The point <math><mi>A</mi></math> is at height <math><mi>h</mi></math> from point <math><mi>B</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The particle slides along the frictionless surface. When the particle reaches point <math><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo></math> its angular momentum about <math><mi>O</mi></math> will be: (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/16332490-194d-48c9-9f6e-5101b99c2166-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 195px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Applying conservation of energy,
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6b03fd6f-73f5-43f6-90dc-b73d17337013-D791.png" style="width: 350px; height: 238px;" />
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></msqrt></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn></msqrt></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Angular momentum about <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math>, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2019 (10 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle which is experiencing a force, given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>, undergoes a displacement of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>. If the particle had a kinetic energy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> at the beginning of the displacement, what is its kinetic energy at the end of the displacement?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Work done by the force,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mover><mi>s</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8901;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

By work energy theorem, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>total</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mn>12</mn><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>15</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q82. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A force acts on a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi></math> object so that its position is given as a function of time as <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo></math> What is the work done by this force in first <math><mn>5</mn></math> seconds?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>875</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>850</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>950</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>900</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Here, the position of the object is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></math>, therefore the velocity of the object will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>d</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mo>d</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p></blockquote><blockquote><p>From the work-energy theorem, all the work done by the force acting on it will be equal to the change in its kinetic energy, i.e.,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>K</mi><mi>E</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>W</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>900</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A block of mass <math><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo></math> lying on a smooth horizontal surface, is attached to a spring (of negligible mass) of spring constant <math><mi>k</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The other end of the spring is fixed, as shown in the figure. The block is initially at rest in its equilibrium position. If now the block is pulled with a constant force <math><mi>F</mi><mo>,</mo></math> the maximum speed of the block is:

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/gkvndhgfanlffeeyiwvnmuwttbylfqtrpnkjygclvmgynvyjiq.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>π</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>k</mi></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/399792_figure_1.png" style="width: 237px; height: 95px;" />

When block attains maximum speed net force on <math><mi>m</mi></math> is zero. <math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></math>

Or <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

From work energy theorem

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>k</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>F</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mi>k</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two particles of the same mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> are moving in circular orbits because of force, given by <math><mi>F</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The first particle is at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, and the second, at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>. The best estimate for the ratio of kinetic energies of the first and the second particle is closest to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>3</mn><mi> </mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>6</mn><mi> </mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>6</mn><mi> </mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>-2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>6</mn><mi> </mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Centripetal force is given as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mfenced><mi>r</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Given in the problem, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>V</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>16</mn><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math>

Kinetic energy is stated as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>K</mi><msub><mi>E</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>16</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> (From equation<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math>)

The ratio of kinetic energies of first and second particle is <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>256</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>17</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>272</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> starts moving from rest along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo></math>axis so that its velocity varies as <math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><msqrt><mi>s</mi></msqrt></math> where <math><mi>a</mi></math> is a constant and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi></math> is the distance covered by the body. The total work done by all the forces acting on the body in the first <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math> second after the start of the motion is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>8</mn><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4</mn><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><msqrt><mi>s</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow><mi>dt</mi></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>d</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">s</mtext></mrow><msqrt><mtext mathvariant="italic">s</mtext></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>.dt</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mi>s</mi></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>s</mi></mrow><mi>dt</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>all</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>all</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>all</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>t</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>all</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>all</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>K</mi></math>

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2018 (08 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A particle is moving in a circular path of radius a under the action of an attractive potential <math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math> Its total energy is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>–</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** Zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>K</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2017 (08 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An object is dropped from a height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>h</mi></math> from the ground. Every time it hits the ground it loses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of its kinetic energy. The total distance covered as <math><mi>t</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>&#8734;</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>∞</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>h</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>h</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since the object is dropped from height, thus its potential energy is equal to its kinetic energy.</p><p>As per question, kinetic energy after hitting the ground is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8242;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>e</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></math>

<math><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

The total distance covered<math><mo>=</mo><mi>h</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A body of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msup><mi>kg</mi></math> is moving in a medium and experiences a frictional force <math> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>k</mi><msup> <mi>v</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>.</mo></mrow> </math> Its initial speed is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math> After <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> its kinetic energy is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>,</mo></math> then value of <math> <mi>k</mi> </math> will be:-

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

After <math> <mrow> <mn>10</mn><mtext>&#8196;</mtext><mtext>sec,</mtext></mrow> </math> <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Acceleration, <math> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>k</mi><msup> <mi>v</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mi>m</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow> <mrow> <mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <mi>k</mi><msup> <mi>v</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mi>m</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>10</mn><mn>5</mn></munderover><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac><mi>k</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>10</mn></munderover><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></math>
<math><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A time dependent force <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>t</mi></math> acts on a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>. If the particle starts from the rest, the work done by the force during the first <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sec</mi></math> will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4.5</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4.5</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi></math>
<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>t</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mi>m</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>t</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>t</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></munderover><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><munderover><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mn>1</mn></munderover><mn>6</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>t</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>f</mi></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4.5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A person trying to lose weight by burning fat lifts a mass of 10 kg upto a height of 1m 1000 times. Assume that the potential energy lost each time he lowers the mass is dissipated. How much fat will he use up considering the work done only when the weight is lifted up? Fat supplies <math><mn>3.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></math> J of energy per kg which is converted to mechanical energy with a 20% efficiency rate. Take <math><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>9.89</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>12.89</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2.45</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>6.45</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>12.89</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>1000</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

Work done against gravity mgh

<math ><mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>98</mn><mtext>&#x2009;</mtext><mi>J</mi></mrow></math>

In lifting 1000 times 

<math><mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#x00D7;</mo><mn>98</mn></mrow></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

Fat burn with 20% efficiency <math><mo>=</mo><mn>3.8</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>0.2</mn></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>7.6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math> per kg

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9.8</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>7.6</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1.289</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>12.89</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

</details>
