### Q91. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A particle is moving in a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> under the action of a force <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> which is directed towards centre of the circle. Total mechanical energy (kinetic energy + potential energy) of the particle is (take potential energy<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>):

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Force <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> will act as centripetal force

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>⇒</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Kinetic energy <math><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>K</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

Also <math><mi>d</mi><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mi>d</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></math>

<math><mo>∆</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mi>d</mi><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mi>α</mi><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>U</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

So total mechanical energy

<math><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>K</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>α</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q92. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>kg</mi></math> is connected to a spring of unknown spring constant k. It is compressed to a distance <math><mi>x</mi> </math> from its equilibrium position and released from rest. After approaching half the distance <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> from the equilibrium position, it hits another block and comes to rest momentarily, while the other block moves with velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The total initial energy of the spring is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.5</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.3</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.6</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

By mechanical energy conservation between compression positions <math><mi>x</mi></math> and <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

On collision with a block at rest

<math><mo>&#8757;</mo></math> Velocities are exchanged <math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> elastic collision between identical masses.

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

### Q93. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>kg</mi></math> rests on a horizontal table. The coefficient of friction between the block and the table is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></math>. When hit by a bullet of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> moving with speed <math><mi>v</mi></math>, that gets embedded in it, the block moves and comes to stop after moving a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> on the table. If a freely falling object were to acquire speed <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> after being dropped from height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi></math>, then neglecting energy losses and taking <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi></math> is close to

 </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math>.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math>.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>km</mi></math>.
- **(D)** None of these.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) None of these.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/image13-51e61d65-0c26-4057-a3d4-a40be0ca8fa7.jpg" />

By the conservation of linear momentum,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

After collision by work energy theorem, we have

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>friction</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>k</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>1</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mi>1</mi></msub></mrow></mfenced><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msubsup><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>0.05</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mn>50</mn><mn>1000</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>10</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mn>50</mn><mn>1000</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>400</mn></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>400</mn></mrow><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>201</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>201</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>201</mn></math>

For a freely falling body,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>H</mi></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>H</mi></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>201</mn></mrow><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mi>g</mi><mi>H</mi></msqrt></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>201</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>H</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mn>201</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>1000</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0.04</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>km</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A small ball of mass $\mathrm{m}$ starts at a point $\mathrm{A}$ with speed $\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{o}}$ and moves along a frictionless track $\mathrm{AB}$ as shown. The track $\mathrm{BC}$ has coefficient of friction $\mu$. The ball comes to stop at $\mathrm{C}$ after travelling a distance $L$ which is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5CJ-N5FBB8GZscyiMqsZo5_M45_ZnyabImCqsL6W4mw.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{2 \mathrm{~h}}{\mu}+\frac{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{o}}^2}{2 \mu \mathrm{g}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mathrm{h}}{\mu}+\frac{\mathrm{v}_0^2}{2 \mu \mathrm{g}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{h}}{2 \mu}+\frac{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{o}}^2}{\mu \mathrm{g}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mathrm{h}}{2 \mu}+\frac{\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{o}}^2}{2 \mu \mathrm{g}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{\mathrm{h}}{\mu}+\frac{\mathrm{v}_0^2}{2 \mu \mathrm{g}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initial speed at point A, $u=v_0$
Speed at point $\mathrm{B}, v=$ ?
$$
\begin{aligned}
&v^2-u^2=2 g h \\
&v^2=v_0^2+2 g h
\end{aligned}
$$
Let ball travels distance ' $S$ ' before coming to rest
$$
\begin{aligned}
&S=\frac{v^2}{2 \mu g}=\frac{v_0^2+2 g h}{2 \mu g} \\
&=\frac{v_0^2}{2 \mu g}+\frac{2 g h}{2 \mu g}=\frac{h}{\mu}+\frac{v_0^2}{2 \mu g}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two springs of force constants $300 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$ (Spring A) and $400 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$ (Spring B) are joined together in series. The combination is compressed by $8.75 \mathrm{~cm}$. The ratio of energy stored in $\mathrm{A}$ and $\mathrm{B}$ is $\frac{E_A}{E_B}$. Then $\frac{E_A}{E_B}$ is equal to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{4}{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{16}{9}$
- **(C)** $\frac{3}{4}$
- **(D)** $\frac{9}{16}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{4}{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given : $\mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{A}}=300 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}, \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{B}}=400 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$
Let when the combination of springs is compressed by force F. Spring A is compressed by $x$. Therefore compression in spring $\mathrm{B}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& x_B=(8.75-x) \mathrm{cm} \\
& \mathrm{F}=300 \times \mathrm{x}=400(8.75-\mathrm{x})
\end{aligned}
$$
Solving we get, $x=5 \mathrm{~cm}$
$$
\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{B}}=8.75-5=3.75 \mathrm{~cm}
$$
$\frac{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{A}}}{\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{B}}}=\frac{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{A}}\left(\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{A}}\right)^2}{\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{k}_{\mathrm{B}}\left(\mathrm{x}_{\mathrm{B}}\right)^2}=\frac{300 \times(5)^2}{400 \times(3.75)^2}=\frac{4}{3}$

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle gets displaced by $\Delta \bar{r}=(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+4 \hat{k}) \mathrm{m}$ under the action of a force $\vec{F}=(7 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k})$. The change in its kinetic energy is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $38 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(B)** $70 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(C)** $52.5 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(D)** $126 \mathrm{~J}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $38 \mathrm{~J}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to work-energy theorem, Change in kinetic energy $=$ work done $=\vec{F} \cdot \Delta \vec{r}=(7 \hat{i}+4 \hat{j}+3 \hat{k}) \cdot(2 \hat{i}+3 \hat{j}+4 \hat{k})$ $=14+12+12=38 \mathrm{~J}$

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The potential energy function for the force between two atoms in a diatomic molecule is approximately given by $U(x)=\frac{a}{x^{12}}-\frac{b}{x^6}$, where a and $b$ are constants and $x$ is the distance between the atoms. If the dissociation energy of the molecule is $D=\left[U(x=\infty)-U_{\text {at equilbrium }}\right], D$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{b^2}{2 a}$
- **(B)** $\frac{b^2}{12 a}$
- **(C)** $\frac{b^2}{4 a}$
- **(D)** $\frac{b^2}{6 a}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{b^2}{4 a}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& U(x)=\frac{a}{x^{12}}-\frac{b}{x^6} \\
& U(x=\infty)=0
\end{aligned}
$$
as, $\quad \mathrm{F}=-\frac{\mathrm{dU}}{\mathrm{dx}}=-\left[\frac{12 \mathrm{a}}{\mathrm{x}^{13}}+\frac{6 \mathrm{~b}}{\mathrm{x}^7}\right]$ at equilibrium, $\quad F=0$
$\begin{array}{ll}\therefore & x^6=\frac{2 a}{b} \\ \therefore & U_{\text {at equiltrium }}=\frac{a}{\left(\frac{2 a}{b}\right)^2}-\frac{b}{\left(\frac{2 a}{b}\right)}=\frac{-b^2}{4 a} \\ \therefore & D=\left[U(x=\infty)-U_{\text {at equilibium }}\right]=\frac{b^2}{4 a}\end{array}$

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An athlete in the olympic games covers a distance of $100 \mathrm{~m}$ in $10 \mathrm{~s}$. His kinetic energy can be estimated to be in the range

**Options:**

- **(A)** $200 \mathrm{~J}-500 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(B)** $2 \times 10^5 \mathrm{~J}-3 \times 10^5 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(C)** $20,000 \mathrm{~J}-50,000 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(D)** $2,000 \mathrm{~J}-5,000 \mathrm{~J}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2,000 \mathrm{~J}-5,000 \mathrm{~J}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Approximate mass $=60 \mathrm{~kg}$
Approximate velocity $=10 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
Approximate $\mathrm{KE}=\frac{1}{2} \times 60 \times 100=3000 \mathrm{~J}$
$\mathrm{KE}$ range $\Rightarrow 2000$ to 5000 joule

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The potential energy of a $1 \mathrm{~kg}$ particle free move along the $\mathrm{x}$-axis is given by
$$
V(x)=\left(\frac{x^4}{4}-\frac{x^2}{2}\right) J
$$
The total mechanical energy of the particle $2 \mathrm{~J}$. Then, the maximum speed (in $\mathrm{m} / \mathrm{s}$ ) is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** $3 / \sqrt{2}$
- **(C)** $\sqrt{2}$
- **(D)** $1 / \sqrt{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3 / \sqrt{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{k} \mathrm{E}_{\max }=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{T}}-\mathrm{U}_{\min }$
$\mathrm{U}_{\min }(\pm 1)=-1 / 4 \mathrm{~J}$
$\mathrm{KE}_{\max }=9 / 4 \mathrm{~J} \Rightarrow \mathrm{U}=\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}} \mathrm{~J}$

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A spherical ball of mass $20 \mathrm{~kg}$ is stationary at the top of a hill of height $100 \mathrm{~m}$. It rolls down a smooth surface to the ground, then climbs up another hill of height $30 \mathrm{~m}$ and finally rolls down to a horizontal base at a height of $20 \mathrm{~m}$ above the ground. The velocity attained by the ball is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $40 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(B)** $20 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(C)** $10 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$
- **(D)** $10 \sqrt{30} \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $40 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{mgh}=1 / 2 \mathrm{mv}^2$
$\mathrm{v}=\sqrt{2 \mathrm{gh}}$
$=\sqrt{2 \times 10 \times 80}=40 \mathrm{~m} / \mathrm{s}$

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A bullet fired into a fixed target loses half of its velocity after penetrating $3 \mathrm{~cm}$. How much further it will penetrate before coming to rest assuming that it faces constant resistance to motion?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3.0 \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(B)** $2.0 \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(C)** $1.5 \mathrm{~cm}$
- **(D)** $1.0 \mathrm{~cm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.0 \mathrm{~cm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$F .3=\frac{1}{2} m v^2-\frac{1}{2} m \frac{v^2}{4}$
$F(3+x)=\frac{1}{2} m v^2$
$x=1 \mathrm{~cm}$

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A particle moves in a straight line with retardation proportional to its displacement. Its loss of kinetic energy for any displacement $\mathrm{x}$ is proportional to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x^3$
- **(B)** $e^x$
- **(C)** $x$
- **(D)** $\log _e x$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x^3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}-\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}}=\frac{\mathrm{mk}}{2} \mathrm{x}^2$
$\mathrm{~K}_{\mathrm{f}}-\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{i}} \propto \mathrm{x}^2$

</details>

### Q105. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A spring of spring constant $5 \times 10^3 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$ is stretched initially by $5 \mathrm{~cm}$ from the unstretched position. Then the work required to stretch it further by another $5 \mathrm{~cm}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $12.50 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{m}$
- **(B)** $18.75 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{m}$
- **(C)** $25.00 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{m}$
- **(D)** $6.25 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{m}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $18.75 \mathrm{~N}-\mathrm{m}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Required work done
$=\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~K}\left(\mathrm{x}_2^2-\mathrm{x}_1^2\right)=\frac{1}{2} \times 5 \times 10^3\left[10^2-5^2\right] \times 10^{-4}$
$=\frac{1}{2} \times 5 \times 75 \times 10^3 \times 10^{-4}=18.75$

</details>

### Q106. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the following two statements:
(A) Linear momentum of a system of particles is zero
(B) Kinetic energy of a system of particles is zero
Then

**Options:**

- **(B)** $A$ implies $B$ but $B$ does not imply $A$
- **(C)** $A$ does not imply $B$ but $B$ implies $A$
- **(D)** $A$ implies $B$ and $B$ implies $A$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $A$ does not imply $B$ but $B$ implies $A$**

No Solution Available

### Q107. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A ball whose kinetic energy is E, is projected at an angle of $45^{\circ}$ to the horizontal. The kinetic energy of the ball at the highest point of its flight will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{E}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E} / \sqrt{2}$
- **(C)** $E / 2$
- **(D)** zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $E / 2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

As the velocity at the highest point reduces to zero. The K.E. of the ball also becomes zero.

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A spring of force constant $800 \mathrm{~N} / \mathrm{m}$ has an extension of $5 \mathrm{~cm}$. The work done is extending it from $5 \mathrm{~cm}$ to $15 \mathrm{~cm}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $16 \mathrm{~J}$
option 1 goes here
- **(B)** $8 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(C)** $32 \mathrm{~J}$
- **(D)** $24 \mathrm{~J}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $8 \mathrm{~J}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{W}=\int_{x_1}^{x_2} F d x=\int_{x_1}^{x_2} K x d x=K\left[\frac{x^2}{2}\right]_{x_1}^{x_2}=\frac{K}{2}\left[x_2^2-x_1^2\right]=\frac{800}{2}\left[(0.15)^2-(0.05)^2\right]=8 \mathrm{~J}$

</details>
